# What Ruby answers, as measured from CRuby.
#
# Every `#=>` in this file was produced by running the snippet on the real Ruby
# named in CI and recording `.inspect`. `scripts/eval-oracle.rb` is what
# re-checks them and `tests/eval.rs` is what makes Spinel agree, so the file is
# falsifiable in both directions: Ruby can disagree with it, Spinel can disagree
# with it, and CI fails on either.
#
# It is a measurement rather than a transcription because several answers here
# are not what a reading of the code would suggest. `-7 / 2` is -4 and not -3;
# `-7 % 2` is 1 and not -1; `!0` is false because 0 is truthy; `1 && 2` is 2
# rather than true. Each of those is a place an interpreter written from
# intuition is quietly wrong.
#
# Format: one snippet per line, its result after `#=>`. Snippets must terminate
# and must not raise — Spinel's *refusals* (a division by zero, a loop that never
# ends) are Rust-side tests, because Ruby's answer there is a value this slice
# deliberately does not have. A bignum used to be on that list; #225 landed it,
# and the rows for it are below.

# ------------------------------------------------------------------ literals
nil  #=> nil
true  #=> true
false  #=> false
42  #=> 42
-7  #=> -7
1.5  #=> 1.5
2.0  #=> 2.0
:sym  #=> :sym
'text'  #=> "text"
[1, :two, 'three']  #=> [1, :two, "three"]
[]  #=> []
[[1], [2, 3]]  #=> [[1], [2, 3]]
()  #=> nil

# -------------------------------------------------------------------- locals
a = 1; a  #=> 1
a = 1; b = 2; a + b  #=> 3
a = (b = 3)  #=> 3
a = 1; a = 2; a  #=> 2

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- arithmetic
# Ruby's `/` and `%` floor. Rust's truncate, so these four are where an
# interpreter that reached for `/` gets it wrong.
7 / 2  #=> 3
-7 / 2  #=> -4
7 / -2  #=> -4
-7 % 2  #=> 1
7 % -2  #=> -1
2 * 3 + 1  #=> 7
1 + 2.0  #=> 3.0
1 == 1.0  #=> true
3 / 2.0  #=> 1.5

# ----------------------------------------------------------------- if/unless
if true then 1 else 2 end  #=> 1
if false then 1 else 2 end  #=> 2
if false then 1 end  #=> nil
unless false then 1 else 2 end  #=> 1
unless true then 1 else 2 end  #=> 2
unless true; end  #=> nil
if true; 'a'; 'b'; end  #=> "b"
a = 2; if a == 1 then :one elsif a == 2 then :two else :other end  #=> :two
a = 1; if (); a = 2; end; a  #=> 1

# ----------------------------------------------------------------- while/until
i = 0; while i < 10; i += 1; end; i  #=> 10
i = 0; until i > 9; i += 1; end; i  #=> 10
i = 0; while i < 3; i += 1; end  #=> nil
i = 0; until i == 1; a = 123; i = 1; end; a  #=> 123
a = 1; while false; a = 2; end; a  #=> 1
a = 1; begin; a = 2; end while false; a  #=> 2
a = 1; begin; a = 2; end until true; a  #=> 2
i = 0; begin; i += 1; end while i < 5; i  #=> 5

# ------------------------------------------------------------- break and next
i = 0; while true; i += 1; break if i == 3; end; i  #=> 3
while true; break 42; end  #=> 42
i = 0; n = 0; while i < 5; i += 1; next if i == 2; n += 1; end; n  #=> 4

# ------------------------------------------------------------------- logical
# `&&` and `||` return an operand, not a boolean.
1 && 2  #=> 2
nil && 2  #=> nil
nil || 2  #=> 2
1 || 2  #=> 1
false || nil  #=> nil
a = 1; false && (a = 2); a  #=> 1
a = 1; true || (a = 2); a  #=> 1
a = nil; a ||= 5; a  #=> 5
a = 1; a ||= 5; a  #=> 1
a = 1; a &&= 5; a  #=> 5
a = nil; a &&= 5; a  #=> nil
a ||= 7; a  #=> 7

# ----------------------------------------------------------------- case/when
case 2; when 1 then :one; when 2 then :two; else :other; end  #=> :two
case 9; when 1 then :one; else :other; end  #=> :other
case 9; when 1 then :one; end  #=> nil
case 3; when 1, 2, 3 then :low; else :high; end  #=> :low
case 'b'; when 'a' then 1; when 'b' then 2; end  #=> 2
a = 5; case; when a < 3 then :small; when a < 10 then :medium; end  #=> :medium
n = 0; case 1; when 1 then n = 1; when 1 then n = 2; end; n  #=> 1

# ------------------------------------------------------------------ equality
'a' == 'a'  #=> true
'a' == 'b'  #=> false
[1, 2] == [1, 2]  #=> true
[1, 2] == [1, 3]  #=> false
[1, [2]] == [1, [2]]  #=> true
[] == []  #=> true
[1] == [1, 2]  #=> false
:a == :a  #=> true
nil == nil  #=> true
nil == false  #=> false
1 != 2  #=> true
a = 'x'; b = 'x'; a == b  #=> true

# ------------------------------------------------------------------- unary
!true  #=> false
!nil  #=> true
!0  #=> false
a = 5; -a  #=> -5

# --------------------------------------------------- methods and their arguments
def m0; 42; end; m0  #=> 42
def m1(a); a; end; m1(7)  #=> 7
def m2(a, b); [a, b]; end; m2(1, 2)  #=> [1, 2]
def opt(a, b = 5); [a, b]; end; opt(1)  #=> [1, 5]
def opt(a, b = 5); [a, b]; end; opt(1, 2)  #=> [1, 2]
def rest(*a); a; end; rest(1, 2, 3)  #=> [1, 2, 3]
def rest(*a); a; end; rest  #=> []
def mid(a, *b, c); [a, b, c]; end; mid(1, 2, 3, 4)  #=> [1, [2, 3], 4]
def mid(a, *b, c); [a, b, c]; end; mid(1, 2)  #=> [1, [], 2]
def kw(a: 1); a; end; kw  #=> 1
def kw(a: 1); a; end; kw(a: 9)  #=> 9
def kwreq(a:); a; end; kwreq(a: 3)  #=> 3
def both(a, b = 2, *c, d, e: 5); [a, b, c, d, e]; end; both(1, 9)  #=> [1, 2, [], 9, 5]
def dflt(a, b = a + 1); [a, b]; end; dflt(4)  #=> [4, 5]
def splat(a, b); [a, b]; end; args = [1, 2]; splat(*args)  #=> [1, 2]
def ret(a); return a; 99; end; ret(3)  #=> 3
def calls(a); inner(a) + 1; end; def inner(a); a * 2; end; calls(5)  #=> 11
# A default that declares a local displaces the parameters after it in Prism's
# scope list, and the binder addresses by position (#164 surfaced this).
def d(a = (b = 1), c = 2); [a, b, c]; end; d  #=> [1, 1, 2]
def d(a = (b = 1), c = 2); [a, b, c]; end; d(9)  #=> [9, nil, 2]
def d(a = (b = 1), c = 2); [a, b, c]; end; d(9, 8)  #=> [9, nil, 8]
def d(a = b = c = 1, e = 2); [a, b, c, e]; end; d  #=> [1, 1, 1, 2]
def d(a = b = c = 1, e = 2); [a, b, c, e]; end; d(3, 4)  #=> [3, nil, nil, 4]
def d(a = (b = 1), c = (e = 2), f = 3); [a, b, c, e, f]; end; d  #=> [1, 1, 2, 2, 3]
def d(a, b = (c = a + 1), e = 2); [a, b, c, e]; end; d(1)  #=> [1, 2, 2, 2]
def d(a, b = (c = a + 1), e = 2); [a, b, c, e]; end; d(1, 5, 6)  #=> [1, 5, nil, 6]
# A repeated underscore still takes a slot of its own and reads the first.
def u(_, _); _; end; u(1, 2)  #=> 1

# --------------------------------------------------------- blocks and yield
def y1; yield 1; end; y1 { |a| a + 1 }  #=> 2
def y0; yield; end; y0 { 7 }  #=> 7
def ym; yield 1, 2; end; ym { |a, b| [a, b] }  #=> [1, 2]
def ya; yield [1, 2]; end; ya { |a| a }  #=> [1, 2]
def ya; yield [1, 2]; end; ya { |a, b| [a, b] }  #=> [1, 2]
def ya; yield [1, 2, 3]; end; ya { |*a| a }  #=> [[1, 2, 3]]
def ya; yield [1, 2, 3]; end; ya { |a = 9| a }  #=> [1, 2, 3]
def ya; yield [1, 2]; end; ya { |a, | a }  #=> 1
def ya; yield [1, 2]; end; ya { |a, b, c| [a, b, c] }  #=> [1, 2, nil]
def yb; block_given?; end; yb  #=> false
def yb; block_given?; end; yb { }  #=> true
def cap; yield; end; x = 10; cap { x }  #=> 10
def cap; yield; end; x = 10; cap { x = x + 5 }; x  #=> 15
def twice; yield; yield; end; n = 0; twice { n = n + 1 }; n  #=> 2
def pass(&b); b.call(3); end; pass { |a| a * 3 }  #=> 9
def fwd; yield 4; end; def outer(&b); fwd(&b); end; outer { |a| a + 1 }  #=> 5

# ------------------------------------------------------ procs and lambdas
l = ->(a) { a + 1 }; l.call(1)  #=> 2
l = -> { 5 }; l.call  #=> 5
l = ->(a, b) { [a, b] }; l.(1, 2)  #=> [1, 2]
l = ->(a) { a }; l[8]  #=> 8
l = ->(a) { a }; l.lambda?  #=> true
p1 = proc { |a, b| [a, b] }; p1.lambda?  #=> false
p1 = proc { |a, b| [a, b] }; p1.call(1)  #=> [1, nil]
p1 = proc { |a, b| [a, b] }; p1.call(1, 2, 3)  #=> [1, 2]
p1 = proc { |a, b| [a, b] }; p1.call([1, 2])  #=> [1, 2]
p1 = proc { |a| a }; p1.call([1, 2])  #=> [1, 2]
l = lambda { |a| a }; l.lambda?  #=> true
->(a) { a }.arity  #=> 1
->(a, b) { a }.arity  #=> 2
->(*a) { a }.arity  #=> -1
->(a, b = 1) { a }.arity  #=> -2
proc { |a| a }.arity  #=> 1
l = ->(a) { return a * 2 }; l.call(4)  #=> 8
n = 1; l = -> { n }; n = 2; l.call  #=> 2

# --------------------------------------------------------------------- send
def s1(a); a * 2; end; send(:s1, 4)  #=> 8
def s1(a); a * 2; end; __send__(:s1, 5)  #=> 10
def sb; yield 2; end; send(:sb) { |a| a + 1 }  #=> 3
def y2; yield 1, 2; end; y2 { [_1, _2] }  #=> [1, 2]
def y1; yield 7; end; y1 { it }  #=> 7
def y1; yield 7; end; y1 { |it| it }  #=> 7
def inner; yield 10; end; def outer; inner { yield 1 }; end; outer { |a| a + 100 }  #=> 101
def inner; yield 1; end; def outer; inner { block_given? }; end; outer { }  #=> true
def inner; yield 1; end; def outer; inner { block_given? }; end; outer  #=> false
def take(&b); b; end; def use; take { yield 2 }; end; use { |a| a * 5 }.call  #=> 10
def f(x, y); [x, y]; end; a = [1, 2]; b = [3]; f(a, *b)  #=> [[1, 2], 3]
def f(*a); a; end; b = [1, 2]; c = [3, 4]; f(*b, *c)  #=> [1, 2, 3, 4]
def f(*a); a; end; b = [2]; f(1, *b, 3)  #=> [1, 2, 3]
def f(x, y, z); [x, y, z]; end; b = [2, 3]; f(1, *b)  #=> [1, 2, 3]
def y; yield 1; end; y { |a| next 9; 5 }  #=> 9
def y; yield 1; end; y { |a| next; 5 }  #=> nil
def y; [yield(1), yield(2)]; end; y { |a| next a * 2 }  #=> [2, 4]
l = ->(a) { next a + 1 }; l.call(1)  #=> 2
(def zz; 1; end)  #=> :zz
def outer2; def inner2; 3; end; end; outer2; inner2  #=> 3
def m; def m; 2; end; 1; end; [m, m]  #=> [1, 2]

# #13 — constants, modules, class bodies, `self`, singleton classes, `defined?`.
#
# Constant lookup order is documented nowhere and reads wrong from `variable.c`,
# so every rule below is a measurement rather than a reading. The two that catch
# people: a lexically enclosing module beats an ancestor, and a qualified `A::X`
# does *not* fall back to `Object` — Ruby removed that in 2.5.
#
# Every case names its own classes. The oracle runs these against one shared
# top level where a constant outlives the line that set it, while Spinel gives
# each line a fresh heap; a case that reused a neighbour's name would compare
# two different programs.

# -- reading and writing ---------------------------------------------------
CW1 = 1; CW1  #=> 1
CW2 = 1; ::CW2  #=> 1
module CW3; end; CW3::B = 2; CW3::B  #=> 2
class CW4; end; CW4::D = 3; CW4::D  #=> 3
CW5 = 1; CW5 = 2; CW5  #=> 2
CW6 = (1 + 2); CW6  #=> 3
class CW7; end; CW7.class  #=> Class
module CW8; end; CW8.class  #=> Module

# -- lexical scope beats the ancestor chain --------------------------------
class LB1; K = "ancestor"; end; module LO1; K = "lexical"; class LI1 < LB1; def self.k; K; end; end; end; LO1::LI1.k  #=> "lexical"
class LB2; K = "ancestor"; end; module LO2; K = "lexical"; class LI2 < LB2; K = "own"; def self.k; K; end; end; end; LO2::LI2.k  #=> "own"
class LB3; K = "ancestor"; end; module LO3; class LI3 < LB3; def self.k; K; end; end; end; LO3::LI3.k  #=> "ancestor"
LT4 = "top"; module LM4; def self.t; LT4; end; end; LM4.t  #=> "top"
module LA5; X = 1; class LB5; def m; X; end; end; end; LA5::LB5.new.m  #=> 1
module LA6; X = 1; class LB6; def self.m; X; end; end; end; LA6::LB6.m  #=> 1
module LA7; X = 1; module LM7; class LC7; def m; X; end; end; end; end; LA7::LM7::LC7.new.m  #=> 1

# -- a block resolves constants where it was written ------------------------
# `yield` rather than `Array#map`: the point is where the block's constants
# resolve, and `map` is #15's.
class BC1; X = 7; def self.each; yield; end; def self.m; each { X }; end; end; BC1.m  #=> 7
module BA2; X = 1; class BB2; def each; yield; end; def m; each { X }; end; end; end; BA2::BB2.new.m  #=> 1

# -- qualified lookup -------------------------------------------------------
class QB1; I = "inherited"; end; class QS1 < QB1; end; QS1::I  #=> "inherited"
module QM2; N = 1; end; QM2::N  #=> 1
# `Object` alone is skipped by a qualified lookup, and only when it is not the
# receiver. `Kernel` and `BasicObject` are searched like any other ancestor —
# narrower than "no fallback", and the difference is a `NameError`.
QT3 = "top"; module Kernel; QK3 = "kernel"; end; class QS3; end; [QS3::QK3, Object::QT3]  #=> ["kernel", "top"]

# -- class and module bodies ------------------------------------------------
class CB1; end  #=> nil
class CB2; 42; end  #=> 42
module CB3; 7; end  #=> 7
class CB4; def m; 1; end; end; CB4.new.m  #=> 1
class CB5; def self.m; 2; end; end; CB5.m  #=> 2
class CB6; def m; 1; end; end; class CB6; def n; 2; end; end; c = CB6.new; [c.m, c.n]  #=> [1, 2]
class CB7; end; class CD7 < CB7; def m; 5; end; end; CD7.new.m  #=> 5
class CB8; def m; 1; end; end; class CD8 < CB8; end; CD8.new.m  #=> 1
class CP9; class Inner; def m; :p; end; end; end; class CQ9 < CP9; class Inner; def m; :q; end; end; end; [CP9::Inner.new.m, CQ9::Inner.new.m]  #=> [:p, :q]
module CN10; end; class CN10::Leaf; def m; 3; end; end; CN10::Leaf.new.m  #=> 3
class CO11; X = 1; end; class CO11; def self.x; X; end; end; CO11.x  #=> 1

# -- `self` -----------------------------------------------------------------
class SC1; def self.who; self; end; end; SC1.who == SC1  #=> true
module SM2; def self.who; self; end; end; SM2.who == SM2  #=> true
class SC3; def who; self; end; end; c = SC3.new; c.who.equal?(c)  #=> true
class SC4; def self.body; self; end; end; SC4.body.equal?(SC4)  #=> true

# -- singleton classes ------------------------------------------------------
class GC1; class << self; def m; 9; end; end; end; GC1.m  #=> 9
class GC2; X = 3; class << self; def m; X; end; end; end; GC2.m  #=> 3
o = Object.new; class << o; def m; 4; end; end; o.m  #=> 4
o = Object.new; class << o; def m; self; end; end; o.m.equal?(o)  #=> true
class GC5; end; class << GC5; def m; 6; end; end; GC5.m  #=> 6
class GC6; def self.a; 1; end; def self.b; a + 1; end; end; GC6.b  #=> 2
class GC7; def self.m; 1; end; end; class GC8 < GC7; end; GC8.m  #=> 1
# A frozen literal is CRuby's fstring and takes no singleton class: the
# immediates' `TypeError`, not the `FrozenError` a `dup.freeze` String gets (#376).
eval("# frozen_string_literal: true\ns = 'y'; begin; def s.m; end; rescue => e; [e.class, e.message]; end")  #=> [TypeError, "can't define singleton"]
eval("# frozen_string_literal: true\ns = 'y'; begin; class << s; end; rescue => e; [e.class, e.message]; end")  #=> [TypeError, "can't define singleton"]
s = "y".freeze; begin; s.singleton_class; rescue => e; [e.class, e.message]; end  #=> [TypeError, "can't define singleton"]
s = "z".dup.freeze; begin; def s.m; end; rescue => e; e.class; end  #=> FrozenError
s = "z".dup.freeze; class << s; self; end.singleton_class?  #=> true
eval("# frozen_string_literal: true\ns = 'abc'; s.instance_eval { size }")  #=> 3
eval("# frozen_string_literal: true\ns = 'abc'; s.instance_exec(2) { |n| size * n }")  #=> 6
eval("# frozen_string_literal: true\ns = 'abc'; s.instance_eval('size')")  #=> 3
eval("# frozen_string_literal: true\ns = 'abc'; begin; s.instance_eval { def q; end }; rescue => e; [e.class, e.message]; end")  #=> [TypeError, "can't define singleton"]

# -- `defined?`: the answer is a table, not a rule ---------------------------
# Only the cases where this heap is the authority. A `defined?` that *misses* is
# `NoDispatch` rather than Ruby's `nil`, because a fresh bootstrap heap cannot
# tell "undefined" from "in a file `require` has not landed to load" — see
# `interp::defined_answer`. Those cases arrive with #39.
defined?(nil)  #=> "nil"
defined?(true)  #=> "true"
defined?(false)  #=> "false"
defined?(self)  #=> "self"
defined?(1)  #=> "expression"
defined?("s")  #=> "expression"
defined?(:s)  #=> "expression"
defined?([1, 2])  #=> "expression"
defined?(1..2)  #=> "expression"
defined?(->{})  #=> "expression"
defined?(if true then 1 end)  #=> "expression"
defined?(while false do end)  #=> "expression"
defined?(1 && 2)  #=> "expression"
defined?(-1)  #=> "expression"
a = 1; defined?(a)  #=> "local-variable"
a = 1; defined?(a = 2)  #=> "assignment"
a = 1; defined?(a = 2); a  #=> 1
defined?(DA1 = 1)  #=> "assignment"
DA2 = 1; defined?(DA2)  #=> "constant"
module DM3; end; defined?(DM3)  #=> "constant"
DA5 = 1; defined?(::DA5)  #=> "constant"
defined?([1, 2, 3])  #=> "expression"
class DC6; def m; 1; end; end; defined?(DC6.new.m)  #=> "method"
class DC8; def self.m; 1; end; end; defined?(DC8.m)  #=> "method"
class DC10; def self.m(*a); 1; end; end; defined?(DC10.m(1))  #=> "method"
def dm11; 1; end; defined?(dm11)  #=> "method"
def dy12; defined?(yield); end; dy12  #=> nil
def dy13; defined?(yield); end; dy13 { }  #=> "yield"
class DC14; def self.boom; raise "never"; end; end; defined?(DC14.boom)  #=> "method"

# --------------------------------------------------- exceptions (#12)
# A raise that nothing catches is a Rust-side test, not a table entry: the
# rule for this file is that a snippet terminates and does not raise. What
# is measured here is what a *handled* raise evaluates to, which is where a
# reading of the code goes wrong — a `begin` with an `else` answers the
# `else`, and one with an `ensure` answers the body, not the `ensure`.
begin; 1; end  #=> 1
begin; 1 / 0; rescue ZeroDivisionError; :caught; end  #=> :caught
begin; 1 / 0; rescue; :bare; end  #=> :bare
begin; 1; rescue; :unreached; end  #=> 1
begin; 1 / 0; rescue TypeError; :wrong; rescue ZeroDivisionError; :right; end  #=> :right
begin; 1 / 0; rescue TypeError, ZeroDivisionError; :either; end  #=> :either
begin; 1 / 0; rescue StandardError => e; e.class; end  #=> ZeroDivisionError
begin; 1; rescue; :r; else; 2; end  #=> 2
begin; 1; ensure; 2; end  #=> 1
begin; 1 / 0; rescue; :r; ensure; :e; end  #=> :r
begin; 1; rescue; :r; else; 2; ensure; 3; end  #=> 2
1 / 0 rescue :modifier  #=> :modifier
r = 0; begin; r = 1; ensure; r = 2; end; r  #=> 2
r = 0; begin; begin; 1 / 0; ensure; r = 5; end; rescue; end; r  #=> 5
r = 0; begin; begin; 1 / 0; rescue; r = 1; end; ensure; r = r + 10; end; r  #=> 11
n = 2; begin; n = n + 1; 1 / (n - 3); rescue ZeroDivisionError; retry; end; n  #=> 4
begin; begin; 1 / 0; rescue TypeError; :inner; end; rescue ZeroDivisionError; :outer; end  #=> :outer
def em1; begin; return 1; ensure; end; end; em1  #=> 1
r = 0; def em2(v); begin; return 1; ensure; v; end; end; em2(9)  #=> 1
begin; raise "boom"; rescue => e; e.message; end  #=> "boom"
begin; raise "boom"; rescue => e; e.class; end  #=> RuntimeError
begin; raise ArgumentError; rescue => e; e.message; end  #=> "ArgumentError"
begin; raise ArgumentError, "why"; rescue TypeError; :no; rescue ArgumentError => e; e.message; end  #=> "why"
begin; raise TypeError.new("made"); rescue => e; e.message; end  #=> "made"
StandardError.new.message  #=> "StandardError"
StandardError.new("x").message  #=> "x"
begin; begin; raise "a"; rescue => e; raise; end; rescue => f; f.message; end  #=> "a"
catch(:tag) { throw :tag, 5 }  #=> 5
catch(:tag) { 7 }  #=> 7
catch { |t| throw t, 3 }  #=> 3
r = 0; catch(:t) { begin; throw :t, 1; ensure; r = 9; end }; r  #=> 9
begin; throw :nope; rescue UncaughtThrowError => e; e.message; end  #=> "uncaught throw :nope"
def bmy; yield 1; yield 2; end; def bm1; bmy { |x| return x * 10 }; :not_here; end; bm1  #=> 10
def bmy2; yield 1; yield 2; :after; end; def bm2; bmy2 { |x| break x * 5 }; end; bm2  #=> 5
def bm3; begin; return 1; ensure; 2; end; end; bm3  #=> 1
def bm4(&b); b.call; end; bm4 { 42 }  #=> 42
r = 0; def bm5; yield; ensure; end; bm5 { 3 }  #=> 3
def dboom; raise "no"; end; defined?(dboom.length)  #=> nil
def dboom2; raise "no"; end; defined?(dboom2)  #=> "method"
[1, 2] + [3]  #=> [1, 2, 3]
[] + []  #=> []
class OpP; def +(other); [:plus, other]; end; end; OpP.new + 7  #=> [:plus, 7]
class OpL; def <(other); :less; end; end; OpL.new < 1  #=> :less
n = 0; m = 0; while n < 3; n = n + 1; m = m + 1; redo if m == 2 && n == 2; end; m  #=> 3
r = 0; n = 0; while n < 2; n = n + 1; begin; next; ensure; r = r + 1; end; end; r  #=> 2
r = 0; n = 0; while n < 5; n = n + 1; begin; break; ensure; r = r + 1; end; end; r  #=> 1
r = 0; n = 0; while n < 5; n = n + 1; begin; break 7; ensure; r = r + 1; end; end  #=> 7
r = 0; begin; n = 0; while n < 2; n = n + 1; next; end; ensure; r = 1; end; r  #=> 1
def ar1(a, b); [a, b]; end; begin; ar1(1); rescue ArgumentError => e; e.message; end  #=> "wrong number of arguments (given 1, expected 2)"
def ar2(a); a; end; begin; ar2(1, 2); rescue ArgumentError => e; e.message; end  #=> "wrong number of arguments (given 2, expected 1)"
def ar3(a, b); [a, b]; end; def ar3c; ar3(1, 2); end; ar3c  #=> [1, 2]
def sp1; yield 1, 2; end; sp1 { |a| a }  #=> 1
def sp2; yield [1, 2]; end; sp2 { |a, b| [a, b] }  #=> [1, 2]
a = 1; defined?((a))  #=> "local-variable"
defined?((1))  #=> "expression"
a = 1; defined?((a = 2))  #=> "assignment"

# `extend`, and the cache invalidation each of these depends on. Every pair
# below asks the same question twice with a definition in between: the first
# answer is cached, and a cache that outlived the definition would repeat it.
module Ex1; def w; "A"; end; end; o = Object.new; o.extend(Ex1); o.w  #=> "A"
module Ex2; def w; "A"; end; end; module Ex3; def w; "B"; end; end; o = Object.new; o.extend(Ex2, Ex3); o.w  #=> "A"
module Ex4; def w; 1; end; end; o = Object.new; before = o.respond_to?(:w); o.extend(Ex4); [before, o.respond_to?(:w)]  #=> [false, true]
module Ex5; extend self; def h; 1; end; end; Ex5.h  #=> 1
# A receiver with no singleton class is refused before frozen is asked: the
# `TypeError`, not `FrozenError`. `nil` extends `NilClass` (#383).
module Ex6; def w; 6; end; end; [1, :a, 1.5, 2**70].map { |o| begin; o.extend(Ex6); rescue => e; [e.class, e.message]; end }.uniq  #=> [[TypeError, "can't define singleton"]]
eval("# frozen_string_literal: true\nmodule Ex9; end; begin; 'y'.extend(Ex9); rescue => e; [e.class, e.message]; end")  #=> [TypeError, "can't define singleton"]
module Ex7; def w7; 7; end; end; [nil.extend(Ex7).w7, true.extend(Ex7).w7, nil.singleton_class.include?(Ex7)]  #=> [7, 7, true]
module Ex8; end; [Object.new.freeze, "z".dup.freeze].map { |o| begin; o.extend(Ex8); rescue => e; e.class; end }  #=> [FrozenError, FrozenError]
class Iv1; end; c = Iv1.new; before = c.respond_to?(:t); class Iv1; def t; 1; end; end; [before, c.respond_to?(:t)]  #=> [false, true]
class Iv2; end; d = Iv2.new; before = d.respond_to?(:t); def d.t; 1; end; [before, d.respond_to?(:t)]  #=> [false, true]
class Iv3; end; e = Iv3.new; before = e.respond_to?(:t); module Iv3M; def t; 1; end; end; class Iv3; include Iv3M; end; [before, e.respond_to?(:t)]  #=> [false, true]
class Iv4; def t; "own"; end; end; f = Iv4.new; before = f.t; module Iv4M; def t; "prepended"; end; end; class Iv4; prepend Iv4M; end; [before, f.t]  #=> ["own", "prepended"]

# --------------------------------------- argument evaluation order (#160)
# Ruby expands a splat at the point it is *written*, before anything to its
# right is evaluated. This convention keeps the array on the stack and expands
# it at the call, so an argument to the right could change what the splat saw.
# Most rows below are cases where the two orders give different answers; the
# last few are the ones that must stay the same.
def ao(*a) = a; x = [1, 2]; ao(*x, x.pop)  #=> [1, 2, 2]
def ao(*a) = a; x = [1, 2]; ao(*x, x.push(9).size)  #=> [1, 2, 3]
def ao(*a) = a; x = [1, 2]; ao(*x, *x)  #=> [1, 2, 1, 2]
def ao(*a) = a; x = [1, 2]; ao(x.pop, *x)  #=> [2, 1]
def ao(*a) = a; x = [1, 2]; ao(*x)  #=> [1, 2]
def ao(*a) = a; x = [1, 2]; ao(*x, 3)  #=> [1, 2, 3]
def aob(*a, &b) = [a, b]; x = [1, nil]; aob(*x, &x.pop)  #=> [[1, nil], nil]
def aob(*a, &b) = [a, b]; x = [1, nil]; aob(*x, 2, &x.pop)  #=> [[1, nil, 2], nil]
def aob(*a, &b) = a; x = [1, 2]; aob(*x) { 3 }  #=> [1, 2]
# A splat of something that is not an Array is passed through unchanged, and
# capturing it must not change that.
def ao(*a) = a; x = 1; ao(*x, 2)  #=> [1, 2]
def ao(*a) = a; x = nil; ao(*x, 2)  #=> [2]

# ------------------------------------------------- method visibility (#161)
# A private method is refused an explicit receiver and found by a receiverless
# call; a protected one is reachable while `self` is a kind of its owner. The
# `NoMethodError` wording differs from the missing-method one above by its
# prefix only, and ruby/spec asserts on both.
class V1; private def m; :m; end; def viaself; self.m; end; def bare; m; end; end; [V1.new.viaself, V1.new.bare]  #=> [:m, :m]
begin; class V2; private def m; end; end; V2.new.m; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.message; end  #=> "private method 'm' called for an instance of V2"
begin; class V3; protected def m; end; end; V3.new.m; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.message; end  #=> "protected method 'm' called for an instance of V3"
class V4; protected def m; :m; end; def peer(o); o.m; end; end; V4.new.peer(V4.new)  #=> :m
class V5; private def m; :m; end; end; V5.new.send(:m)  #=> :m
begin; class V6; private def m; end; end; V6.new.public_send(:m); rescue NoMethodError => e; e.message; end  #=> "private method 'm' called for an instance of V6"
begin; class V7; protected def m; end; def go; public_send(:m); end; end; V7.new.go; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.class; end  #=> NoMethodError
# The predicates. `method_defined?` is public-or-protected; the second argument
# is `inherit`, not `respond_to?`'s `include_all`.
class V8; def a; end; private def b; end; protected def c; end; end; [V8.method_defined?(:a), V8.method_defined?(:b), V8.method_defined?(:c)]  #=> [true, false, true]
class V9; def a; end; private def b; end; protected def c; end; end; [V9.public_method_defined?(:a), V9.private_method_defined?(:b), V9.protected_method_defined?(:c)]  #=> [true, true, true]
class VA; def a; end; private def b; end; end; [VA.public_method_defined?(:b), VA.private_method_defined?(:a)]  #=> [false, false]
class VB; def m; end; end; class VC < VB; end; [VC.method_defined?(:m), VC.method_defined?(:m, false)]  #=> [true, false]
class VD; def a; end; private def b; end; end; o = VD.new; [o.respond_to?(:a), o.respond_to?(:b), o.respond_to?(:b, true)]  #=> [true, false, true]
# Where the scope visibility applies, and where it stops.
class VE; private; def m; :m; end; end; VE.private_method_defined?(:m)  #=> true
class VF; private; def m; end; end; class VF; def n; :n; end; end; VF.new.n  #=> :n
class VG; private; [1].each { def m; end }; end; VG.private_method_defined?(:m)  #=> true
class VH; private; def outer; def nested; :n; end; :o; end; end; h = VH.new; h.send(:outer); h.nested  #=> :n
class VI; private; def self.s; :s; end; end; VI.s  #=> :s
class VJ; def a; end; def b; end; end; [(class VJ; private :a; end), (class VJ; private :a, :b; end)]  #=> [:a, [:a, :b]]
begin; class VK; private :nope; end; rescue NameError => e; e.message; end  #=> "undefined method 'nope' for class 'VK'"
class VL; def m; :base; end; end; class VM < VL; private :m; end; VL.new.m  #=> :base
# `module_function`: private instance method, public singleton copy. This is
# what makes the two `defined?` answers differ.
[defined?(Kernel.puts), defined?(Object.print)]  #=> ["method", nil]
module VN; def f; :f; end; module_function :f; end; VN.f  #=> :f
module VO; def f; end; module_function :f; end; VO.private_method_defined?(:f)  #=> true
class VP; private def m; end; def probe; [defined?(self.m), defined?(m)]; end; end; VP.new.probe  #=> [nil, "method"]
class VQ; private; attr_accessor :a; end; VQ.private_method_defined?(:a)  #=> true
# A top-level `def` is a private instance method of `Object`.
def toplevel_private_probe; :t; end; Object.private_method_defined?(:toplevel_private_probe)  #=> true

# ---------------------------------------------- a missing method raises (#170)
# A failed dispatch is an ordinary Ruby raise, so a `rescue` catches it. Before
# #170 it was a Rust-level error that unwound past every handler, and none of
# the rows below could be written at all.
#
# The message is not one wording: `nil`, `true`, `false`, a class, and a module
# each read differently from an ordinary instance. That is measured here rather
# than transcribed, because three of the five are text a spec asserts on and a
# reading of CRuby's `error.c` is exactly where the wrong one comes from.
#
# Anonymous receivers are absent on purpose: CRuby writes the object's address
# into the message, so no table can hold the answer.
begin; Object.new.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.class; end  #=> NoMethodError
begin; Object.new.nope; rescue => e; e.class; end  #=> NoMethodError
begin; Object.new.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.is_a?(NameError); end  #=> true
begin; Object.new.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.is_a?(StandardError); end  #=> true
begin; Object.new.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.message; end  #=> "undefined method 'nope' for an instance of Object"
begin; 1.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.message; end  #=> "undefined method 'nope' for an instance of Integer"
begin; :s.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.message; end  #=> "undefined method 'nope' for an instance of Symbol"
begin; nil.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.message; end  #=> "undefined method 'nope' for nil"
begin; true.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.message; end  #=> "undefined method 'nope' for true"
begin; false.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.message; end  #=> "undefined method 'nope' for false"
begin; Object.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.message; end  #=> "undefined method 'nope' for class Object"
begin; Comparable.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.message; end  #=> "undefined method 'nope' for module Comparable"
begin; Object.new.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.name; end  #=> :nope
begin; Object.new.nope(1, 2); rescue NoMethodError => e; e.name; end  #=> :nope
o = Object.new; begin; o.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.receiver.equal?(o); end  #=> true
begin; nil.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.receiver; end  #=> nil
begin; 7.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.receiver; end  #=> 7
begin; Object.nope; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.receiver.equal?(Object); end  #=> true
# The raise is a value like any other: it unwinds out of a method, and an
# `ensure` on the way still runs.
def m170; Object.new.nope; end; begin; m170; rescue NoMethodError => e; e.name; end  #=> :nope
r170 = []; begin; begin; Object.new.nope; ensure; r170.push(1); end; rescue NoMethodError; r170; end  #=> [1]
begin; Object.new.nope; rescue NoMethodError; "caught"; else; "no"; end  #=> "caught"
# `respond_to?` still answers without raising, which is what the guards in
# ruby/spec lean on.
Object.new.respond_to?(:nope)  #=> false

# ------------------------------------------------- range, hash, array splat
(1..3).begin  #=> 1
(1...3).end  #=> 3
(1..3).exclude_end?  #=> false
(1...3).exclude_end?  #=> true
(1..3).to_a  #=> [1, 2, 3]
(1...3).to_a  #=> [1, 2]
(3..1).to_a  #=> []
(1..3).first  #=> 1
(1..3).last  #=> 3
(1..5).first(2)  #=> [1, 2]
(1..5).last(2)  #=> [4, 5]
(1..5).cover?(0)  #=> false
(1..5).cover?(5)  #=> true
(1...5).cover?(5)  #=> false
((1..5) === 3)  #=> true
((1..) === 99)  #=> true
((..5) === -1)  #=> true
(1..).end  #=> nil
(..5).begin  #=> nil
(1..5).inspect  #=> "1..5"
(1...5).inspect  #=> "1...5"
(1..).inspect  #=> "1.."
(..5).inspect  #=> "..5"
(nil..nil).inspect  #=> "nil..nil"
(1..5).to_s  #=> "1..5"
((1..5) == (1..5))  #=> true
((1..5) == (1...5))  #=> false
{}.size  #=> 0
{}.inspect  #=> "{}"
# A Symbol key is written as a label where its spelling can be one, quoted as
# a label where it cannot, and the hashrocket stays for every other key. The
# rule is the Symbol's spelling, not its class, and this block is its table.
{b: 1, "s" => 2, 3 => 4}.inspect  #=> "{b: 1, \"s\" => 2, 3 => 4}"
{"odd key": 1, "1": 2, "": 3, nil => 4}.inspect  #=> "{\"odd key\": 1, \"1\": 2, \"\": 3, nil => 4}"
[:+, :-, :[], :[]=, :==, :<=>, :!, :"?", :-@].map { |k| {k => 1, 2 => 3}.inspect }  #=> ["{\"+\": 1, 2 => 3}", "{\"-\": 1, 2 => 3}", "{\"[]\": 1, 2 => 3}", "{\"[]=\": 1, 2 => 3}", "{\"==\": 1, 2 => 3}", "{\"<=>\": 1, 2 => 3}", "{\"!\": 1, 2 => 3}", "{\"?\": 1, 2 => 3}", "{\"-@\": 1, 2 => 3}"]
[:a?, :a!, :A?, :_?, :a=, :"a?b", :"a!?", :"a??", :"a?="].map { |k| {k => 1, "a" => 2}.inspect }  #=> ["{a?: 1, \"a\" => 2}", "{a!: 1, \"a\" => 2}", "{A?: 1, \"a\" => 2}", "{_?: 1, \"a\" => 2}", "{\"a=\": 1, \"a\" => 2}", "{\"a?b\": 1, \"a\" => 2}", "{\"a!?\": 1, \"a\" => 2}", "{\"a??\": 1, \"a\" => 2}", "{\"a?=\": 1, \"a\" => 2}"]
[:@ivar, :@@cvar, :$gvar, :$1, :Const, :_, :__, :a_1, :"9a", :"a-b", :"a.b", :":a", :"\n"].map { |k| {k => 1, 1.5 => 2}.inspect }  #=> ["{\"@ivar\": 1, 1.5 => 2}", "{\"@@cvar\": 1, 1.5 => 2}", "{\"$gvar\": 1, 1.5 => 2}", "{\"$1\": 1, 1.5 => 2}", "{Const: 1, 1.5 => 2}", "{_: 1, 1.5 => 2}", "{__: 1, 1.5 => 2}", "{a_1: 1, 1.5 => 2}", "{\"9a\": 1, 1.5 => 2}", "{\"a-b\": 1, 1.5 => 2}", "{\"a.b\": 1, 1.5 => 2}", "{\":a\": 1, 1.5 => 2}", "{\"\\n\": 1, 1.5 => 2}"]
[:nil, :if, :self, :class, :end, :BEGIN, :__FILE__, :__method__].map { |k| {k => 1}.inspect }  #=> ["{nil: 1}", "{if: 1}", "{self: 1}", "{class: 1}", "{end: 1}", "{BEGIN: 1}", "{__FILE__: 1}", "{__method__: 1}"]
[:é, :Été, :"é?", :"aあ", :"\u{1F600}", :"a b"].map { |k| {k => 1}.inspect }  #=> ["{é: 1}", "{Été: 1}", "{é?: 1}", "{aあ: 1}", "{😀: 1}", "{\"a b\": 1}"]
begin; e = Encoding.default_external; Encoding.default_external = Encoding::US_ASCII; ["\u3042".inspect, :"\u3042".inspect, {"\u3042": 1}.inspect, "\u{1F600}".inspect, "\u3042".inspect.encoding.name]; ensure; Encoding.default_external = e; end  #=> ["\"\\u3042\"", ":\"\\u3042\"", "{\"\\u3042\": 1}", "\"\\u{1F600}\"", "US-ASCII"]
begin; e = Encoding.default_external; Encoding.default_external = Encoding::ISO_8859_1; ["\x85".force_encoding("ISO-8859-1").inspect, "\u3042".inspect]; ensure; Encoding.default_external = e; end  #=> ["\"\\x85\"", "\"\\u3042\""]
begin; Encoding.default_internal = Encoding::US_ASCII; ["\u3042".inspect, :"\u3042".inspect, {"\u3042": 1}.inspect]; ensure; Encoding.default_internal = nil; end  #=> ["\"\\u3042\"", ":\"\\u3042\"", "{\"\\u3042\": 1}"]
[eval("'\xE9'".b).encoding.name, eval("'a'".force_encoding("US-ASCII")).encoding.name, eval("x = '\xE9'\n[x.bytes, __LINE__]".force_encoding("ISO-8859-1"), nil, "f", 10)]  #=> ["ASCII-8BIT", "US-ASCII", [[233], 11]]
({b: 1}.to_s)  #=> "{b: 1}"
{1 => {b: 1}, [:c] => {"d" => :e}}.inspect  #=> "{1 => {b: 1}, [:c] => {\"d\" => :e}}"
(h = {}; h[:x] = h; h.inspect)  #=> "{x: {...}}"
(h = {a: 1}; h[:b] = [{c: h}]; h.inspect)  #=> "{a: 1, b: [{c: {...}}]}"
